2.       RECOGNITION 
A. Troy School District’s Outstanding Teacher of the Year Awards 
B. Mini Grant Awards – Michigan Schools & Government Credit 
Union 
This evening we are pleased to recognize our Teacher of the Year 
nominees and recipients and those who received mini grant 
awards from the Michigan Schools and Government Credit Unio n.  
Earlier tonight , we had a short reception prior to the Board 
meeting t o celebrate these accomplishments.  I would like to 
thank the credit union for sponsoring the welcome reception. 
At this time, I would like to introduce our director of community 
relations, Kerry Birmingham, to begin our recognition program.   
Following our recognition of the Teacher of the Year nominees, 
Sally Gibson, vice president of business development for the 
credit union, will recognize the mini grant recipients.

     2. A.      4.19.11 
2. RECOGNITION 
A.  Oakland County Outstanding Teacher of the Year Awards  
The Troy School District has many outstanding teachers on its staff. This 
evening we are proud to recognize t he accomplishments of sixteen  individuals 
who were nominated for the Troy School District  Outstanding Teacher Award 
Program. 
The sixteen nominees represent exemplary educators at every level  and were 
nominated by their peers, by parents and by former and current students , all 
of whom talked about the impact these teachers have on the lives of those 
around them. Their nomination letters described how the professionals we 
honor here tonight help students find their voices, their passion and their love 
for learning. Perhaps the best tribu te came from a former student of one of 
the nominees who said “I will carry with me the lessons I learned from this 
teacher everywhere I go.” 
Would you please come forward as your name is called? (Distribute 
certificates/plants.) 
Three of our district no minees are sent to Oakland Schools to represent Troy 
in the county -wide Outstanding Teacher Award Program. Due to the criteria 
set by Oakland Schools, Caryn Leonard, fourth-grade teacher at Barnard 
Elementary School was nominated at the elementary level; Ewa Ostrowski , 
ESL teacher from Smith Middle School and Trevor Smith, Physics teacher at 
Troy High School was nominated at the high school level.  Mrs. Leonard was 
also selected as the district’ s overall Teacher of the Year and awarded a free 
one-year lease of a car, donated through the generosity of the Suburban 
Collection.  The three candidates were chosen by a TEA selection committee 
to represent the District.   
At this time, I would like to invite Mrs. Leonard, Mrs. Ostrowski and Mr. Smith 
to say a few words.

Schools of Choice – Section 105 
Education  of  the  Troy  School  District,  exercising  the 
option permitted by Section 105 and Section 105c of the 
State  School  Aid  Act  of  1979,  as  amended  by  Public  Act 
300 of 1996, will accept applications for a limited Schools 
of  Choice  program  for  the  2011/2012  school  year  for 
nonresident students who attend another school district 
within  Oakland  County  (Section  105)  or  if  the  student 
attends a district in another county, if that intermediate 
school  district  is  contiguous  with  the  Oakland 
Intermediate  School  District  boundaries  (Section  105c) 
for  the  purposes  of  attending  grades  6  –  8  at  Baker 
Middle School.        
Comments

Choice  application  period.  Dr.  Fowler  informed  Mr. 
Miletti  of  the  high  expectations  in  the  IB  program.   She 
stated  that  there  would  be  testing  and  counseling  for 
students  who  apply  to  the  program  to  ensure  that  only 
the  most  serious  students  are  accepted.   It  is  projected 
that the District will have 25 students registering.

Budget Reduction Plan for 
said that the District has been addressing financial issues 
reductions since 2002.   She stated that in 2010/2011 the 
district made $6.2 million in reductions and in 2009/2010 
reductions were $6.7 million.  She stated that the District 
is facing a very severe deficit next year and that no one is 
happy  about  recommending  any  of  the  proposed  cuts.  
Dr.  Fowler  also  mentioned  Governor  Snyder’s  intention 
to  use  monies  from  the  School  Aid  Fund,  which  is 
intended  for  K ‐ 12  education,  and  put  it  towards 
community colleges and higher education programs.  She 
urged the community members to pay attention to what 
is happening in Lansing and to make their voices heard in 
keeping public schools funded.   
Mr.  Rajter  presented  a  report  on  the  proposed  budget 
reduction  plan  for  the  2011/2012  school  year.   He 
commented that the proposed reductions total just over 
$4 million and that the District’s initial target was to cut 
$6  million  from  the  budget  but  after  hearing  the 
Governor’s  announcement  on  cuts  the  District  will  need 
to  make  $8.4  million  in  cuts.   He  also  stated  that  this 
information  will  be  presented  at  the  upcoming 
Community Budget Meetings.   
Kinderplus  to  Kindersession.   Dr.  Fowler  responded  that 
the Kinderplus program currently has a certified teacher 
in  the  morning  plus  a  teacher  aide  in  the  afternoon  for 
the child care portion.  Dr. Fowler stated that the District 
proposing  to  offer  Kindersession,  which  would  bring  the 
tuition cost  for parents down from  $3,000 to  $2,500 for 
the school year. 
Ms.  Philippart  thanked  Mr.  Rajter  for  the  presentation.  
She  encouraged  and  welcomed  the  community  and 
members of the audience to attend the upcoming Budget 
reductions.   She  also  stated  that  the  Board  and  District 
administration  would  be  interested  and  open  to  hear 
other  considerations  or  ideas  on  what  other  areas  the

Vietnam veteran, and Troy police officer, announced that 
the VFW and TPD have joined efforts in the past to send 
Trotter, which is owned by the VFW.  Last year they were 
able  to  send  15  kids  to  camp  at  $180  per  child  from 
Sunday  to  Sunday.   He  stated  that  the  children  couldn’t 
stop  talking  about  the  incredible  time  they  had.   The 
activities  offered  at  the  camp  are  something  less‐
fortunate  kids  would  never  have  the  opportunity  to 
experience.   The TPD and VFW would like to try sending 
kids to camp again.   They are holding a spaghetti dinner 
fundraiser  this  Friday  at  the  VFW  4037  on  Maple  Rd.  in 
Troy  from  5:00  –  8:00  p.m.   The  cost  is  $8  per  person.  
The  VFW  is  also  accepting  donations  for  this  cause  – 
checks  can  be  made  payable  to  Troy  VFW  Post  #4037.   
Their  goal  this  year  is  to  send  30  kids  to  camp  and  the 
Troy  Coalition  and  Youth  Assistance  will  be  teaming  up 
with  them.   Mr.  Kerr  presented  the  Board  with  an 
invitation to the event.

raised.

can  log  into  the  Athens’  website  and  schedule  their

adults, $10 for students, and $5 for seniors and patrons.

Athens  students  who  received  scholarships  signed 
commitments to schools such as University of Iowa, EMU 
and  many  others.   March  25th  the  Athens  orchestra  will 
be  performing  at  Carnegie  Hall  –  they  are  so  proud  of 
their orchestra and the great honor they have received.   
Mrs. Edmunds commented that she and her family were

of  Oz”  and  that  it  was  a  good,  wholesome  play  that 
should  be  enjoyable  for  families.   Mrs.  Edmunds  also 
encouraged  parents  to  see  Troy  High’s  presentation  of 
“Seussical”,  which  is  geared  specifically  for  children  and 
grandkids.  She said the costumes were great and the Dr. 
Seuss character did a great job.

       ADMINISTRATION BUILDING

Philippart,  the  following  Board  members  were  present:   Mrs.  Edmunds,  Mrs.  Fleming,

7. CURRICULUM 
A. REPORT:  Troy Teachers Technology Training Study   
The District was approached in the Fall of 2010 , about conducting a 
study of technology usage within the district, in partnership with 
Oakland University School of Business Administration and Consumer 
Insights, Inc., a Troy -based market research firm.  Assistant 
Superintendent for Secondary Instruction , Dr. Rich Machesky , and 
Director of Technology, Scott Bryan, decided to investigate the impact of 
our Smart Board initiativ e through this opportunity.  Tonight we wanted 
to present the exciting findings of this report and how the results are 
helping to determine future training offerings for teachers.  
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This is a report.   
Karl Schmidt, President of Consumer Insights, Inc. and Scott Bryan will 
present this evening’s report.

Study Backgroundy g
• Oakland University School of Business Administration offers a senior‐level research 
methods course through its Organizational Behavior department.
• As part of this coursework, the instructor (Karl Schmidt, of Consumer Insights, Inc.) 
sought out a partner to conduct a comprehensive research project that could 
expose his students to a real‐world organizational challenge.
• An initial contact with Rich Machesky and subsequent referral to Scott Bryan• An initial contact with Rich Machesky and subsequent referral to Scott Bryan 
provided internal cooperation for a technology training effort assessment within 
the Troy School District.
• With Scott’s leadership and strong support, we were able to conduct a full p g pp ,
qualitative and quantitative research effort among teachers across the TSD.
• Students analyzed and presented their interpretation of the data to the instructor, 
but this deck represents a primary analysis of the data by Karl Schmidt.
• No names were collected as part of the research, but to assure anonymity for all 
teachers who participated in the study, the TSD administration will receive data 
only in summary form; individual survey records with any identifying teacher 
statistics will be held in strict confidentiality at Consumer Insights Inc
statistics will be held in strict confidentiality at Consumer Insights, Inc.
T4 Study Final Report Chart 3
171
Current Snapshot: Smart Board Use in TSDp
• 367 classrooms out of 600 have functioning SMART Boards—installed in equal three 
waves in January 2009, August 2009, and August 2010.
• Installation strategy was to distribute across all schools and grade levels.
• To date, teachers have had to apply for SMART Boards in their classrooms, including a 
plan for how they intend to use the equipment.
h d b h l ( ff) h• Most teachers were trained by Smart Tech personnel (not TSD TRC staff) within a 
month of installation and then during a follow‐up session about two months later .
• Initial Smart Tech training had not occurred for all new Fall 2010 installations at the 
time the survey was conductedtime the survey was conducted.
• TRC maintains a Smart Board Moodle site, and online course work is available from 
the vendor .
• Teachers do not receive any paper training materials or a manual for the equipment.Teachers do not receive any paper training materials or a manual for the equipment.
• District budget constraints will not allow future training to be conducted during the 
school day if that requires substitute teachers.
T4 Study Final Report Chart 4
172
Study Objectives and Methodologyy j gy
• This study tackled six strategic questions concerning implementation of Smart 
Board technology within the Troy School District:
• How are teachers at different levels and in different subjects using Smart 
Board technology in their classrooms?
• What do they see as the biggest benefits of the technology, and how could it 
be made better?
• How would teachers assess their Smart Board training and support 
experiences to date within the district?
• Wh t St Bd kl d till it T th ?• What Smart Board knowledge gaps still exist among Troy teachers?
• What would be the most valuable focus for future training and support

Future Training Strategies
Future Training Strategies
Teachers view their SMART Board as an excellent addition to 
th i l bt th h t fl l tdtheir classroom—but a resource they have not fully tapped. 
• Both in the discussions as well as in the quantitative survey, teachers attribute to 
the SMART Board improvements in the quality of their lectures and the “uptake” 
of relevant course material by their students.
• Those who have undergone training to date walk away with a basic understanding 
of the equipment but are craving more applied training:of the equipment, but are craving more applied training:
• Examples and demonstrations tied to their specific grade and curriculum
• The ability to practice and execute examples shown in formal training before 
they leave that training sessionthey leave that training session
• Basic set‐up and execution of Senteos, as well as a software framework for 
integrating them into their lesson plans
• A way of sharing specific ideas with colleagues across the District.y g p g
T4 Study Final Report Chart 22
190
Recognizing budget and staff resource constraints, there are still many 
steps that the TRC can take to improve basic training moving forward p p g g f
and develop ongoing training for active SMART Board users.
• The TRC should work with SMART Tech trainers in an effort to better structure 
initial training on the system:
• Slow down the pace of first training session.
• Provide opportunities for students to practice system functions.
• Use classroom‐relevant examples—even provide “case studies” from other 
Troy teachers.
• If possible, segment training by elementary, middle school, and high school 
teachers (with applications and examples catered to each group)teachers (with applications and examples catered to each group)
• To date, teachers are finding tools and applications haphazardly; we need to shift 
from a general search engine/individual discovery mentality to a specific 
resource/group‐shared mentality./g p y
• The TRC can encourage this shift by providing a resource “link list” that lists 
many of the sources we evaluated in this survey with a description of what 
types of resources can be attained from each site.
• Ideally, TRC would also incorporate a mechanism for TSD teachers to 
contribute new sites as they are discovered.
T4 Study Final Report Chart 23
191
Other training improvements to consider…g p
• Fix the TSD SMART Board Moodle site:
• Incorporate the “essentials” as identified in the study wherever possible.
• Perhaps more importantly, reposition the site as a dynamic collaboration 
source rather than static central hub.
• If resources are available (perhaps as a high school student project?) develop a 
series of short “how‐to” animated PowerPoint decks that tackle specific aspects of 
SMART Board equipment and software.
• Senteo training could be executed using this method• Senteo training could be executed using this method.

upper grade teachers, who may think of these as elementary student tools.
• Provide 1‐hour “10 best” style seminars as options during September professionalProvide 1 hour  10 best  style seminars as options during September professional 
development days to keep teachers fresh and engaged with new software and 
applications as they become available.

8. A.     04.19.11

A. CONSIDERATION:  Authorization to Implement Budget Reduction  
                                       Plan 
The current school funding crisis is impacting every district in Michigan. Despite 
the fact that the Troy School District has been fiscally responsible by making 
more than $5 7.9 million in budget adjustments since 2002, it is necessary to 
continue to reduce expenditures and enhance revenue to eliminate deficit 
spending in future years. The budget reductions for the 2011-2012 school year 
were presented to the Board at the March 1, 2011, Board of Education Meeting.  
Following that meeting, four community forums were held to receive input and 
answer questions from our school community.  All comments were reviewed by 
the Board.  The District administration is  requesting the Board to consider a 
resolution to authorize staff  to begin implement ation of  those reductions as 
presented.  These changes are necessary to protect the financial future of our 
schools. The following re solution is presented to the Board of Education for 
consideration. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the resolution below. 
  Moved by:      __________________________  
  Supported by:  __________________________  
 Whereas depending on the final approved School Aid Act, the Troy School 
District will be facing an operating deficit in the 201 1-2012 school year estimated to be 
between $13.6 million and $16 million as a result of projected revenue cuts and rising 
costs; and 
Whereas it is clear that the ongoing school funding crisis will continue to impact 
Michigan districts, and the Troy School District must make budget adjustments to 
protect the District’s long -term stability and promote educational excellence for its 
students; and 
Whereas the Board of Education expects to pursue a three -year plan of utilizing 
the District’s fund equity and making budget adjustments ;  
Therefore be it resolved  that the Troy School District Board of Education 
hereby authorizes the administration to begin taking the ne cessary actions to 
194
implement approximately $6 million in budget adjustments and draw on existing fund 
equity of approximately $7.6 million to address the projected deficit in the 201 1-2012 
school year. 
Therefore be it further resolved  that the Board of Education will continue to 
work with its community, parents, staff, bargaining units and state lawmakers to 
continue to advocate for our students and the resources necessary to maintain and 
enhance our exemplary programs.  
 Ayes _______ 
 Nays _______

 B.  CONSIDERATION:  Reductions in Certified Staff  
It is recommended that  due to the projected financial condition of the School 
District and the proposed budget reductions  for the 201 1/2012 fiscal year, the 
Board of Education approve the reduction of certified staff included on the 
attached list consistent with the applicable provisions of the Michigan Teachers’ 
Tenure Act and the criteria set forth in the collective bargaining agreement 
between the District and the T roy Education Association.  Accordingly, the 
teachers on the attached list shall be notified in writing of layoff effective at the 
end of the day on June 30, 2011.

Memorandum 
 DATE: April 14, 2011 
 TO: Dr. Barbara A. Fowler, Ph.D. 
  Superintendent 
Human Resources 
Department 
 FROM: Jasen M. Witt, J.D. 
  Assistant Superintendent, Human Resources 
4400 Livernois 
Troy, MI 48098 
Fax - 248/823-4014 
As you are aware, per the collective bargaining agreement between TSD and the TEA, 
teachers shall be notified of voluntary layoff no later than May 1 st.  Given the 
complicated nature of the staffing process , the desire to limit the involuntary transfer of 
more senior staff members,  and the budget constraints which have led to the 
recommended reduction of 14 FTE  in certified staff for the 2011/2012 school  year, it is 
necessary to provide  some teachers with an initial layoff notification which would 
become effective June 30, 2011.  
Due to the number of teacher retirements and resignations to date – a total of 20, I am 
pleased that we are able to recommend a minimal number of teachers – a total of 10 – 
to receive notification of layoff.  Furth er, I believe that the majority , if not all,  of teachers 
who receive initial notification of layoff will be recalled  to a teaching assignment  
(although most likely a different assignment)  prior to the start of the 2011/2012 s chool 
year – if not before June 30, 2011.  
This recommendation, if approved, would provide formal notification of layoff to the 10 
teachers hired by the District on or after August 16, 2010.  The names of these 10 
teachers – along with the name of one Baker Middle School teacher (Ms. Kara 
Spodarek) whose contract is being recommended for non -renewal as a result of her 
unsatisfactory performance – are included on the attached list.  (Note: Absent our 
receipt of a resignation from Ms. Spodarek prior to the Ap ril 19, 2011, Board meeting, 
consideration of the non -renewal of her teaching contract will be included under the 
General – Consent Agenda.)  This list of teacher names will not be read at the Board 
meeting, but will be incorporated by reference in the rec ommendation for consideration.  
I will work with Dr. Machesky, Mrs. Keeling and the building administration to see that 
the individuals on this list are provided notice  of layoff in advance of the Board meeting.  
Formal written notification of layoff , effective June 30, 2011, would follow the approval of 
this item by the Board.  In addition, upon approval, we will begin to work with the TEA   
to complete the staffing process in a timely manner which will allow for us to recall  as 
many teachers who have been initially notified of layoff as possible.  
If you have any questions regarding the notifications of layoff and/or the 
recommendation for the non -renewal of Ms. Spodarek’s contract due to her overall 
unsatisfactory performance, please let me know.  
197

                      Copy Paper 
On February 17, 2011 bids were opened for copy paper for district wide usage.  Bids 
were sent to 17 potential bidders with 12 responding.  The original re commendation 
relating to this bid was to award to D & P Management in the amount of $50,400.00  As 
noted in my earlier memo, I am recommending that this bid be rescinded due to D & P 
Management’s inability to fulfill the contract.  
In reviewing the next lo w bid (tied between Rock LLC and Sovereign Paper LLC), it was 
noted that neither company could honor their pricing due to subsequent changes in the 
market. 
The next low bidder, RIS Paper, confirmed that they would still honor their quote for the 
original pricing and quantity.  RIS Paper is a company that Troy School District has 
favorably done business with in the past.  
One difference, of note, is that subsequent to the bid opening, RIS Paper has changed 
names and is now known as Contract Paper Group Inc.  
I recommend award of this bid to Contract Paper Group Inc. totaling $63,579.60.  
Please include this recommendation as an action item on the April 19, 2011 Board 
agenda. 
201

 D.  CONSIDERATION:  Awarding of Bid for Copy Paper  
 On February 17, 2011, bids were opened for copy paper for district -
wide usage.  Bids were sent  to 17 potential bidders with 12 
responding ( none of which were “No Bid”).  The original 
recommendation awarded this bid to D & P Management on March 1,

10. A.    04.19.11 
10. OTHER 
A.  CONSIDERATION:  Resolution Opposing School Aid Fund Transfer  to Higher   
                    Education 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
  Moved by:     
  Supported by:     
RESOLUTION OPPOSING SCHOOL AID FUND 
TRANSFER TO HIGHER EDUCATION 
 WHEREAS, well-educated students prepared for college and careers is the best investment 
Michigan can make in the pursuit of a strong economy and a better future for its residents; and 
 WHEREAS, Michigan’s standards for high school graduation have never been higher, with all 
students completing the Michigan Merit Curriculum and Exam to prepare them for post -secondary 
education or training; and 
 WHEREAS, school funding h as increased by less than 1 percent per year during the past 
decade, failing to keep up with increased costs, mandates and standards, prompting staff and budget 
reductions to maintain programming; and  
 WHEREAS, Michigan voters in 1994 approved Proposal  A, a measure that eliminated the 
ability of school districts to seek operating funds from constituents and shifted that responsibility to 
the State through an increase in the sales tax; and  
 WHEREAS, the ballot language of Proposal A clearly indicated those revenues would be 
totally dedicated to public schools, as it was crafted to provide tax relief for property owners while 
maintaining adequate funding for K-12 education; and 
 WHEREAS, community colleges and institutions of higher education were not included in the 
ballot language of Proposal A, nor were they mentioned in voter communication stating the intent of 
the constitutional amendment; and  
 WHEREAS, community colleges and insti tutions of higher education are afforded the 
opportunity to increase tuition and fees to finance operations while school districts are prohibited 
from seeking additional operating funds to compensate for inadequate state funding; and  
204
 WHEREAS, the t ransfer of funds from the School Aid Fund to community colleges and 
institutions of higher education will debilitate the School Aid Fund and its ability to adequately fund 
public schools; now    
 THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED,  the Troy School District oppose s the proposal to divert 
dollars from the School Aid Fund to support community colleges and institutions of higher education 
at the expense of public school districts and the children they serve.  
Ayes:      
Nays:      
Adopted this 19th day of April, 2011 
Nancy Philippart, President 
Troy Board of Education 
Ida Edmunds, Vice President  Paula Fleming, Secretary
